# OT2001 Term Continuity Note

## 1. Scope and Chronology Cursor

The opening authorities are the [23 canonical Holdings volumes](../../../state/holdings/INDEX.md), their synchronized [continuous view](../../../state/HOLDINGS.md), [Standards and Tests](../../../state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md), and [Standing State](../../../state/STANDING_STATE.md). All three tracker headers identify last completed October Term 2000, processed through June 28, 2001, after all 114 completed OT2000 Court events, edition November 9, 2026. Standing State identifies opening October Term 2001. These repository publications govern the opening baseline.

The divergence point is October 7, 1991, when Chief Justice Alex-Lamar Stone-Zsela replaces Rehnquist. [Court Composition](../../../foundation/COURT_COMPOSITION.md) governs roster, seniority and allotments. No vacancy or oath-date condition applies at this opening.

The [manifest](manifest.md) retains all 127 inventory rows and both opening carryovers. 112 scheduled Court events in chunks 1–6 and chunk 7 are completed; 15 inventory matters remain open. The latest processed event date is 2002-06-20. All uncoordinated matters on a shared date use the common entering-law baseline immediately before that date, including across chunk boundaries. Processing and authorship assignment order create no legal priority. Later uncoordinated matters sharing the latest event date use that date's common preceding baseline. Romeu remains completed at its February 19 calendar position; its prospective equality relief operates from that date.

The next eligible scheduled group is 2002-06-20: United States v. Angle. Those uncoordinated matters use the baseline immediately before that date. Opinion assignments separately follow effective date and alphabetical case order.

The requested redistricting injunction is denied. The Court orders no replacement plan, election change or new preclusion hearing. The separate residency count and proceeding are unaffected. | [Record](../records/robertson-v-bartels_summary_2002-06-20.md) |
| 2002-06-20 | Rush Prudential HMO, Inc. v. Moran | Affirmed, 6–3; insured independent medical review saved and enforceable through ERISA benefits relief. | The Seventh Circuit's judgment for Moran stands. The affirmed relief enforces the insured benefit with the statutory physician determination; the Court orders no further merits proceedings. | [Record](../records/rush-prudential-hmo-inc-v-moran_merits_2002-06-20.md) |
| 2002-06-20 | Utah v. Evans | Affirmed, 6–3; standing unanimous, statutory permission 7–2, constitutional validity six with Thomas opposed and O'Connor/Scalia reserving. | The direct appeal concludes in affirmance. The challenged 2000 count and allocation remain operative. The Court orders no seat transfer, replacement dataset, presidential injunction or new census. Review of materially different or unlawfully implemented methods remains governed by the applicable statutory and constitutional standards. | [Record](../records/utah-v-evans_merits_2002-06-20.md) |

## 3. Current Law

The opening Holdings and Standards and Tests remain authoritative at their published scope, as affected by the following effective current-term law. Exact controlling propositions and precedent treatment remain in the linked public-law sections and authoritative Records.

### Brown v. City of Oneonta — 2001-11-13

Purposeful investigative selection by race is subject to the common substantial-justification standard. Victim-supplied identifying information may justify fitted measures, but its source and the absence of hostility create no categorical exemption. The rule governs the classification and its justification; individual liability and remedies remain subject to their existing requirements.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#brown-v-city-of-oneonta-merits-2001-11-13).

### Nebraska v. Wyoming — 2001-11-13

The complete modified decree governs the parties' allocation, accounting, storage, groundwater treatment, administration and reserved rights. Its express future-effective and modification provisions control their respective subjects. Existing general enforcement and modification law remains; the settled allegations supply no independent new entitlement.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#nebraska-v-wyoming-decree-2001-11-13).

### TRW Inc. v. Andrews — 2001-11-13

The governing version of §1681p has no general discovery extension. Its precise statutory misrepresentation exception remains available when its conditions are met. The Court's ruling does not decide the first-presented actual-loss theory or create a universal accrual rule for federal statutes.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#trw-inc-v-andrews-merits-2001-11-13).

### United States Postal Service v. Gregory — 2001-11-13

A pending contractual grievance creates no categorical statutory bar to considering earlier discipline in later adverse-action review. Adequate independent review remains essential, and the agency's proof burden is separate from judicial review. Bolling's actual adequacy remains undecided by the Court. A completed reversal must receive the effect required by the applicable agreement and review framework through the available procedural mechanisms.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-postal-service-v-gregory-merits-2001-11-13).

### Adarand Constructors, Inc. v. Mineta — 2001-11-27

No substantive doctrinal change. The governing racial-classification law remains in force. The lower-court judgment survives without a Supreme Court endorsement of its constitutional rationale.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#adarand-constructors-inc-v-mineta-dig-2001-11-27).

### Chickasaw Nation v. United States — 2001-11-27

Section 2719(d)(1) does not give covered tribal gaming chapter 35's state wagering-tax exemption. Reporting and withholding parity operates within the provision's described subject. Other Code exclusions, the actual tax base and the occupational tax's independent linkage remain governed by their own terms; no general tribal federal-tax immunity is recognized.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#chickasaw-nation-v-united-states-merits-2001-11-27).

### Church of Scientology International v. Behar — 2001-11-27

The First Amendment supplies no automatic subsidiary-meaning immunity for a materially distinct defamatory factual accusation. Context may establish absence of actionable material falsity, and the public-figure burden remains demanding and statement-specific. Protected criticism elsewhere in a publication cannot replace those inquiries. State-law injury, causation and damages rules remain separately operative.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#church-of-scientology-international-v-behar-merits-2001-11-27).

### Correctional Services Corp. v. Malesko — 2001-11-27

A confined federal constitutional damages cause is available against a private corporation exercising federal custody for its own deliberate constitutional misconduct through policy, established practice or authorized decision. Corporate attribution and causation must be proved; ordinary negligence and respondeat superior do not suffice. Meyer's federal-agency bar and existing individual-action rules remain in force.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#correctional-services-corp-v-malesko-merits-2001-11-27).

### J. E. M. Ag Supply, Inc. v. Pioneer Hi-Bred International, Inc. — 2001-12-10

Plants meeting the utility statute's requirements may receive utility patents alongside the plant-specific regimes. Each right carries its own requirements and exceptions. The certificate amendments and Asgrow's surviving former-law operation retain their actual reach; the decision supplies no generalized saved-seed or research exemption from utility-patent rights.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#j-e-m-ag-supply-inc-v-pioneer-hi-bred-international-inc-merits-2001-12-10).

### Schrader v. Blackwell — 2001-12-10

The official party designation may remain linked to Ohio's recognition system in these circumstances. The holding applies burden-and-fit review to a designation restriction; it supplies no categorical approval of every recognition threshold or prohibition of truthful affiliation.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#schrader-v-blackwell-merits-2001-12-10).

### United States v. Knights — 2001-12-10

The Fourth Amendment permits the defined probation-condition and individualized-suspicion combination despite ordinary investigative purpose. Existing scope, execution and exclusion rules retain their separate operation; the decision creates no general suspicionless-search or consent-waiver rule.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-v-knights-merits-2001-12-10).

### Dusenbery v. United States — 2002-01-08

A known prisoner must receive notice through a reasonably calculated custodial route. The proved certified-mail and internal-distribution process satisfies that standard without proof of actual receipt. The holding is procedure-based and leaves genuinely unreliable or known-failure situations to their own records.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#dusenbery-v-united-states-merits-2002-01-08).

### Great-West Life & Annuity Insurance Co. v. Knudson — 2002-01-08

Section 502(a)(3) does not authorize this demand for personal payment merely from the reimbursement clause. Genuine property-based equity remains available when its defendant, property and defense requirements are met. A different claim against another holder requires its own proper pleading, jurisdiction, process and equitable basis; the present judgment decides no such claim.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#great-west-life-annuity-insurance-co-v-knudson-merits-2002-01-08).

### Searles v. Van Bebber — 2002-01-08

Original section 1997e(e) bars the named distress compensation absent physical injury while preserving nominal recognition and punitive relief under their own predicates. Established liability and punitive culpability survive a separate damages-calculation defect. The lawful punitive amount and consequential fee decision remain ordinary remedial work under those settled rules.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#searles-v-van-bebber-merits-2002-01-08).

### Toyota Motor Manufacturing, Kentucky, Inc. v. Williams — 2002-01-08

The original ADA now requires the stated severe restriction in central daily-life manual tasks and permanent or long-term impact, assessed individually under the existing underlying-impairment mitigation rule. Occupational categories alone do not establish disability; workplace and nonwork functional evidence remain relevant. The lower courts apply those settled rules to the reviewed partial judgment, with other claims retaining their existing procedural status.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#toyota-motor-manufacturing-kentucky-inc-v-williams-merits-2002-01-08).

### Chao v. Mallard Bay Drilling, Inc. — 2002-01-09

OSHA retains authority over these working conditions because Coast Guard potential authority was not exercised through pertinent standards or comprehensive regulation. Work on a barge in Louisiana falls within §4(a), subject independently to any displacement actually established under §4(b)(1). Actual overlap, rather than vessel status alone, governs other settings.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK1_PUBLIC_LAW.md#chao-v-mallard-bay-drilling-inc-merits-2002-01-09).

### Kelly v. South Carolina — 2002-01-09

Effective January 9, 2002, the combined future-danger presentation triggers Simmons despite its concurrent character and prison-adaptability uses, and the actual defense argument and ordinary-meaning charge are constitutionally inadequate. The existing contextual trigger, effective alternative means of explanation and State-burden direct-review harmlessness standard remain in force. The danger and adequacy determinations bind the remand; the state court must perform the distinct error-effect inquiry. No controlling rationale requires fixed words or treats every omission as structural error.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#kelly-v-south-carolina-merits-2002-01-09).

### Riley v. St. Luke's Episcopal Hospital — 2002-01-09

The FCA's bounded assigned-claim mechanism may constitutionally continue after initial governmental declination. Its private claimant does not occupy a continuing federal office, and the actual retained government powers, with their distinct statutory conditions, are sufficient for the arrangement presented. These Article II conclusions are separate from assigned-injury standing and statutory defendant coverage. They authorize no general private executive office and do not decide an actual judicial override of a supported government request absent from this record.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#riley-v-st-lukes-episcopal-hospital-merits-2002-01-09).

### EEOC v. Waffle House, Inc. — 2002-01-15

The FAA supplies no bar for this excluded employment agreement. Independently, an employee's private forum promise does not remove Congress's individual-remedy categories from the EEOC's own enforcement action, even if the promise binds the employee under an independent source. Liability and every actual award remain governed by their statutory predicates, mitigation rules and nonduplication limits. Concrete adjudication, release, settlement and satisfaction questions await a record establishing them.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#eeoc-v-waffle-house-inc-merits-2002-01-15).

### In re Cybernetic Services, Inc. — 2002-01-15

A nonownership patent security interest otherwise perfected under the governing California law needs no additional section 261 filing to prevail against this section 544(a)(1) trustee. Transaction coverage and protected-transferee status remain independent federal inquiries. Both former California withdrawal provisions retain transaction-specific operation and do not replace state perfection with a federal ownership register that lacks a perfection system for this interest. The Court leaves actual ownership-transfer and purchaser contests to their own preserved facts and statutory analysis.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#in-re-cybernetic-services-inc-merits-2002-01-15).

### Thomas v. Chicago Park District — 2002-01-15

Genuinely neutral park coordination may use the ordinance's objective grounds, bounded administrative timetable, written reasons, correction and alternative-scheduling provisions, and review without requiring the District to initiate a censorship proceeding after each denial. Permissive forgiveness of a listed defect does not itself create facially unbounded discretion. The same structure remains subject to meaningful review and to properly presented challenges to viewpoint discrimination, actual delay or other unauthorized administration. No automatic-issuance rule or universal court-decision deadline is established.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#thomas-v-chicago-park-district-merits-2002-01-15).

### United States v. Arvizu — 2002-01-15

Supported observations capable of innocent explanations retain their actual contextual force but gain no automatic positive weight by accumulation. Independent judicial review determines whether their relationships supply individualized suspicion. This pre-stop combination meets that standard without neighborhood stigma, proof of an observed offense or post-stop contraband. The lawful-stop conclusion leaves the independent authority for the ensuing search to the preserved consent questions.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-v-arvizu-merits-2002-01-15).

### National Cable & Telecommunications Assn., Inc. v. Gulf Power Co. — 2002-01-16

Section 224's general coverage encompasses qualifying mixed cable-Internet and wireless telecommunications attachments. A gap or dispute about an applicable rate formula does not itself create a jurisdictional exclusion. The statutory formulas, ownership and provider limits, and qualifying state-regulation allocation retain their force. These are new coverage holdings; no Internet-service classification or particular rate is approved.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#national-cable-telecommunications-assn-inc-v-gulf-power-co-merits-2002-01-16).

### Kansas v. Crane — 2002-01-22

The serious-difficulty requirement is a new controlling due-process rule for the civil-commitment question presented. Courts must examine actual functional impairment connected to the qualifying condition and dangerous conduct, while retaining the applicable statutory burden. The functional rule governs application on remand; whether the existing verdict and evidence satisfy it is factual and remedial work, not unresolved law. Separate retrospective-punishment claims retain their own demanding proof and temporal requirements.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#kansas-v-crane-merits-2002-01-22).

### Lee v. Kemna — 2002-01-22

The adequate-state-ground doctrine now expressly reaches this exceptional application of Missouri's continuance requirements. Useful, regularly applied procedural requirements remain enforceable, but their ordinary legitimacy does not make an exorbitant application adequate. Removal of this particular default barrier leaves the due-process merits, remaining lawful habeas restrictions and entitlement to relief for their own adjudication.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#lee-v-kemna-merits-2002-01-22).

### United States v. Microsoft Corp. — 2002-01-22

The presented platform/browser combination receives a restriction-specific rule-of-reason inquiry, with existing tying categories preserved outside this setting and the case's unpresented-claim limits retained. Section 455(a)'s appearance rule remains objective. Civil relief from an established violation depends on all three Liljeberg interests; on this record they require a new judge to assess and reconsider material liability foundations where meaningful independence cannot be established, beyond replacement of the remedial decree. The factual competitive balance, individual findings' binding status and appropriate relief are remand applications of those rules.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-v-microsoft-corp-merits-2002-01-22).

### Barnhart v. Sigmon Coal Co. — 2002-02-19

Direct asset succession to an original signatory is insufficient by itself for the challenged statutory assignments. Independently qualifying related persons, the separate prospective assignment-transfer mechanism and enforceable contractual obligations remain governed by their own conditions. This is a statutory coverage rule, not a general immunity for asset purchasers or a constitutional invalidation of the program.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#barnhart-v-sigmon-coal-co-merits-2002-02-19).

### Igartua De La Rosa v. United States — 2002-02-19

Courts may enforce applicable constitutional rights in Puerto Rico, but may not order this territorial presidential-elector appointment and count without a constitutional allocation. The present allocation differs from equal administration of an existing State's electorate. The judgment resolves the preserved claim and leaves the unappealed and outside-scope questions in their existing posture.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#igartua-de-la-rosa-v-united-states-merits-2002-02-19).

### Owasso Independent School District No. I-011 v. Falvo — 2002-02-19

Temporary peer grading and immediate reporting in the described instructional process do not constitute institutional maintenance of education records. FERPA coverage requires both relation to the student and the statutory maintenance function. Record location is not a categorical condition; later institutional retention and the private-enforcement question remain distinct.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#owasso-independent-school-district-no-i-011-v-falvo-merits-2002-02-19).

### Romeu v. Cohen — 2002-02-19

Souter's Parts II, III and V, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy and Ginsburg, control reversal of the federal and state equality dismissals. An otherwise comparably qualified former resident completely excluded from presidential participation by relocation to Puerto Rico may invoke electoral equality within New York's retained former-resident presidential mechanism. Once that comparison and exclusion are established, the government must show narrow tailoring to a compelling electoral interest. The asserted membership, administrative and incremental-accommodation interests fail at this scope. The Fifth Amendment governs the federal protection and the Fourteenth governs the state exclusion; territorial residence is not suspect and no alternative rational-basis holding is adopted.

Anderson–Burdick review is extended to that preserved opportunity, retaining its calibrated scrutiny. McDonald is distinguished because its record did not establish total voting exclusion; Timmons and Schrader retain their meaningful-access holdings. Harper and Reynolds support equality within the available franchise; Carrington preserves valid residence rules without a permanent franchise; McPherson preserves state electoral allocation. Harris remains at its benefits holding. Saenz and the prior Bush holdings retain their source-specific reach.

Part I has all nine joins: the recurring prospective dispute remains reviewable, the two claims remain distinct, and UOCAVA's minimum does not prohibit broader state eligibility or itself answer federal inequality. Part IV has all eight Associates' joins: travel, Article IV and general due process establish no independent permanent former-state entitlement. Stone-Zsela does not reach Part IV. This is not an additional partial affirmance, and no controlling rationale requires a narrower-ground construction.

The remand requires a prospective federal declaration and state injunction against the respective responsible officials, ordinary qualification findings and implementation. Both forms of relief address the departure-linked domicile exclusion as applied; the state decree also prevents denial because of Puerto Rico residence, abode or domicile, registration or local voting. Ordinary former-residence, age, registration and authentication requirements and prevention of a second presidential vote remain. Local territorial voting is not a duplicate presidential vote. Federal overseas minimum obligations remain; relief creates no territorial electors, changes no other State's program and reopens no completed election. Future program amendments, territorial statehood, congressional representation and territorial electors are outside the decided questions.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#romeu-v-cohen-merits-2002-02-19).

### Winn-Dixie Stores, Inc. v. Commissioner — 2002-02-20

The four-of-seven exception is not a statutory safe harbor from substantive-sham scrutiny. This arrangement's accepted lack of both nontax business purpose and pretax profit potential supports the disallowance. Transactions with meaningful economic substance, mixed findings or an affirmative statutory incentive remain governed by their own legal and factual predicates.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#winn-dixie-stores-inc-v-commissioner-merits-2002-02-20).

### Wisconsin Department of Health and Family Services v. Blumer — 2002-02-20

States may use the income-first method in the section 1396r-5(e)(2)(C) resource-allowance hearing while respecting ownership attribution, the optional character of transfer, and the actual-availability condition for a posteligibility deduction. A lawful forecast is not an accomplished transfer. Resource inadequacy, exceptional financial duress and court-ordered support retain their separate statutory treatments.

Exact holdings, proposition-level authority, precedent treatment, limits and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#wisconsin-department-of-health-and-family-services-v-blumer-merits-2002-02-20).

All uncoordinated matters on the same date use the common entering-law baseline immediately before that date, including across chunk boundaries. Processing or assignment order does not make another same-day decision entering law. No coordinated same-day sequence is established by the supplied neutral scheduling fields. Conditional references to Leventhal in Drayton and to Harris in Ring do not establish priority. Any later established coordination requires an express, validated sequence.

Companion and related-matter references identify scheduling relationships only. A later-calendar matter is unavailable as entering law until its own event date; proposed positions and historical dispositions supply no current-term holding. Earlier current-term law enters only through validated, effective Records. The same limits apply to dependencies elsewhere in the neutral packets.

### Porter v. Nussle — 2002-02-26

From February 26, 2002, individualized episodes of excessive force fall within section 1997e(a). The actual-availability and responsive-process conditions remain. The decision changes no grievance deadline or procedural-default rule and creates no free-standing futility exception; those matters are outside the categorical question reviewed.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#porter-v-nussle-merits-2002-02-26). [Record](../records/porter-v-nussle_merits_2002-02-26.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The case returns through the Second Circuit for application of the exhaustion condition and the stated dismissal rule. Any genuinely preserved availability objection is resolved on supported facts. The constitutional force claim proceeds only through the sequence permitted by the governing statute.

### Swierkiewicz v. Sorema N.A. — 2002-02-26

From February 26, 2002, the ordinary Federal Rules govern these employment-discrimination complaints without a compulsory McDonnell Douglas checklist. Pleading sufficiency permits the case to proceed to the appropriate next stage; liability and evidentiary sufficiency remain governed by their distinct substantive and procedural rules.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#swierkiewicz-v-sorema-na-merits-2002-02-26). [Record](../records/swierkiewicz-v-sorema-na_merits_2002-02-26.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The case returns through the Second Circuit with dismissal set aside for ordinary proceedings on the pleaded Title VII and ADEA claims. The lower courts apply the ordinary procedural rules as the case develops.

### Guest v. Leis — 2002-02-27

From February 27, 2002, restricted bulletin-board subscriber information receives the stated personal, account-specific inquiry; entrustment alone is not a categorical forfeiture. Six Justices support that rule for confidential credentials and five support its stated broader identifying-record scope. The eight-Justice objective immunity holding preserves the existing personal defense and its conduct-date inquiry. A newly recognized constitutional application does not itself defeat that defense, and personal immunity supplies no institutional defense.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#guest-v-leis-merits-2002-02-27). [Record](../records/guest-v-leis_merits_2002-02-27.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The Sixth Circuit judgment is affirmed on the reviewed individual-capacity subscriber claims. The affected institutional and official-capacity subscriber claims return for personal privacy, actual acquisition, subscriber-specific lawful warrant scope, defendant capacity, applicable source of liability, policy or authorized-decision attribution, causation and preserved defenses under the stated rules. The other adjudicated branches remain undisturbed. The remand does not itself require a trial where an independently sufficient legal or undisputed factual ground resolves a claim.

### Raygor v. Regents of University of Minnesota — 2002-02-27

Section 1367(d)'s linked tolling protection excludes the specified nonconsenting State's immune supplemental state-law claims. The independent federal ADEA channel remains valid, and immunity remains claim-specific. The decision rests on statutory coverage, leaving constitutional power to enact a differently specified tolling rule undecided because that issue is unnecessary to this judgment.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#raygor-v-regents-of-university-of-minnesota-merits-2002-02-27). [Record](../records/raygor-v-regents-of-university-of-minnesota_merits_2002-02-27.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The state limitations judgment stands, and this merits review concludes. The final federal dismissal remains undisturbed. Ordinary mandate procedure applies.

### Delaney v. DeTella — 2002-03-04

The binding conditions standard reaches knowing, unreasonable maintenance of the described prolonged exercise deprivation. On those assumed facts the personal defense fails under the law existing in 1996. Individual knowledge, actual response, authority, causation and recoverable injury remain matters for ordinary adjudication; no universal exercise timetable or blanket lockdown exception is created.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#delaney-v-detella-merits-2002-03-04). [Record](../records/delaney-v-detella_merits_2002-03-04.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The Seventh Circuit's legal denial of qualified immunity is affirmed. The case returns for ordinary proceedings concerning each official's knowledge, response, authority and causal responsibility, and the injury and other predicates of the remedy sought. Proper later dispositive motions remain available. Factual disputes excluded from this interlocutory review receive no appellate resolution.

### New York v. FERC / Enron Power Marketing, Inc. v. FERC — 2002-03-04

Independent interstate transmission serving retail purchasers falls within the FPA transmission grant, subject to the retained facilities, entity, state-function and retail-wheeling boundaries. Section 206 remains mandatory upon its stated hearing and unlawfulness finding. This order's separate determination that compulsory retail unbundling was unnecessary sustains its limited remedy; neither the possession of regulatory power nor Enron's preference makes that additional remedy compulsory here. The Court establishes no permanent bundled-transmission exemption or general allocation of bundled-service jurisdiction.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK3_PUBLIC_LAW.md#new-york-v-ferc-enron-power-marketing-inc-v-ferc-merits-2002-03-04). [Record](../records/new-york-v-ferc-enron-power-marketing-inc-v-ferc_merits_2002-03-04.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The D.C. Circuit's judgment is affirmed in Nos. 00-568 and 00-809. The reviewed open-access requirements continue in force, and the request for compulsory additional unbundling in this proceeding is denied. This merits review concludes without an agency remand. Any later proceeding must rest on its own lawful authority, findings and explanation.

### Ruiz v. United States (Ruiz v. Johnson) — 2002-03-04

Prospective prison-decree duties remain subject to the separate statutory termination grounds and amended preservation test. ### Sao Paulo State of the Federative Republic of Brazil v. American Tobacco Co. — 2002-04-01

[Record](../records/sao_paulo_v_american_tobacco_summary_2002-04-01.md)

### Mistaken attribution and continuing organizational leadership

**Holding and operative rule:** Section 455(a) does not require disqualification where a judge was mistakenly identified as an association's president in a motion to file an amicus brief, remained on its executive committee, took no part in preparing or approving the submission, and was only vaguely aware of the earlier case. The informed observer must weigh that genuine leadership role together with the established limits of participation; on these combined facts, the attribution does not reasonably call impartiality into question.

**Authority:** The per curiam opinion is joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine adopt the same statutory rule, application and reversal with remand.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that the appearance inquiry must use the full facts. Liteky supplies the objective standard: an informed, detached observer considers the relevant circumstances together, without requiring proof of actual bias. Liljeberg likewise requires an informed assessment and keeps the existence of a violation distinct from the remedy for one. The mistaken presidential designation cannot be treated as true, but the judge's continuing executive-committee membership cannot be disregarded either.

Organizational leadership can connect a person to an institutional position even without personal authorship. That is the defendants' strongest argument. Here, however, the established absence of preparation or approval and the judge's vague awareness materially limit what the observer can attribute to the judge. Treating this particular brief's advocacy as the judge's own despite those facts would give the organizational affiliation decisive weight it does not bear in this record. The conclusion rests on the objective circumstances, not the judge's assurance of fairness.

Microsoft applies the same standard to supported outside conduct concerning live merits, credibility and relief. Those undisclosed discussions created a materially different relationship to the litigation. The distinction concerns the actual conduct and context; neither press contact nor intentional wrongdoing is a prerequisite to recusal. This decision creates no general exemption for organizational leaders or for people who did not write a submission. Actual participation, responsibility for an organization's position, financial interests and other supported relationships remain subject to the ordinary appearance standard. The circuit's disqualification ruling therefore cannot stand on this ground.

- Liteky v. United States, 510 U.S. 540 (March 7, 1994): applies the controlling cumulative, informed-observer standard. The absence of actual bias does not defeat an appearance claim, and no additional intensity requirement governs this outside affiliation.
- Liljeberg v. Health Services Acquisition Corp., 486 U.S. 847 (1988): applies its informed appearance inquiry and preserves the separate remedial assessment after a violation is established. Risks of injustice to the parties, injustice in other cases and harm to public confidence neither create an otherwise absent violation nor excuse an established one.
- United States v. Microsoft Corp. (January 22, 2002): distinguishes its supported undisclosed discussions about the live litigation from the limited connection to advocacy established here. Its objective appearance rule and separate treatment of civil relief remain in force.

Section 455(a)'s objective appearance standard remains unchanged. On the established combination of mistaken attribution, continuing committee leadership, no preparation or approval and vague awareness, disqualification is not required. The holding protects neither organizational affiliation categorically nor actual advocacy from ordinary appearance review. Liljeberg's remedial inquiry remains distinct from whether a violation exists.

The decision concerns this replacement boulevard route and extends no access order to the casino interior or unrelated private property.

### US Airways, Inc. v. Barnett — 2002-04-29

[Record](../records/us_airways_v_barnett_merits_2002-04-29.md)

**Common authority:** Justice Breyer's Opinion of the Court is joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor and Kennedy. Those five direct joins control every holding below. The separate writings qualify or oppose the rule only to the extent expressly stated; no combination of dissenting grounds supplies additional authority.

### Reasonable accommodation and undue hardship perform separate statutory work

**Holding and operative rule:** An employee ordinarily satisfies the initial accommodation showing by identifying an accommodation reasonable on its face or in the run of cases, not merely one effective for that employee; once reasonableness is shown, the employer must demonstrate significant difficulty or expense in its particular operations under section 12111(10). A requested exception to a disability-neutral policy is not automatically outside the accommodation duty, and the expectation that another worker may obtain an open position under this noncontractual policy does not by itself make the position unavailable for the statutory inquiry.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that reasonable accommodation may require different treatment to provide an employee a workplace opportunity. Section 12111(9) expressly identifies possible reassignment to a vacant position and adjustment of policies. Its permissive examples do not make every requested preference reasonable, but they foreclose treating uniform enforcement alone as an answer. Nor does this policy's bidding expectation establish an incumbent who must be displaced: Barnett occupies the position, the employer declares it open for bidding, and the policy creates no contractual entitlement.

Reasonable is an independent limit. An effective change may affect fellow employees in ways not captured by the employer's own operational expense. The ordinary first showing and the employer's particular hardship defense therefore do different work. This allocation does not require the employee to disprove every possible business burden. Garrett preserves the enacted distinction between accommodation and the employer's demonstrated significant-difficulty-or-expense defense. The latter considers the accommodation's nature and cost; facility resources, staffing, expenses and operational impact; the entity's overall resources, size and facilities; and workforce functions and the facilities' geographic, administrative and fiscal relationships.

The contrary disability-created-obstacle theory would deny a seniority exception because the allocation applies to all workers. But the present accommodation responds to Barnett's inability to perform his former work, and reassignment and policy adjustment remain statutory possibilities. Reasonableness limits those possibilities without excluding the requested exception at the outset. This conclusion also rejects effectiveness alone as a complete test; the employer's actual seniority system must receive the independent consideration stated next.

**Precedent treatment:** Garrett's separation of statutory conditions is preserved. Sutton, Murphy and Toyota retain their distinct coverage rules; none converts coverage into a right to a particular accommodation.

### An established seniority system ordinarily defeats a conflicting request, subject to special circumstances

**Holding and operative rule:** An employer's showing that a requested reassignment conflicts with an established seniority system ordinarily establishes that the accommodation is unreasonable and warrants summary judgment on that issue, including when management created the system. The employee may defeat that ground by presenting evidence of special circumstances making an exception reasonable in the particular case; the employee bears that showing, and the employer retains the separate undue-hardship defense if reasonableness is established.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that established seniority carries weight beyond its possible financial cost. Objective ordering gives employees expectations of fair treatment, job security and predictable advancement. Requiring the employer in every ordinary case to justify whether those expectations should yield would substitute discretionary accommodation decisions for the consistency on which the system depends. Those benefits can exist under a management-created policy as well as a collective agreement. Hardison supplies an analogy concerning the importance of seniority, not its different statute's hardship threshold or an automatic ADA exemption.

The absence of an express ADA seniority exemption does not eliminate the word reasonable. Conversely, the independent force of reasonable does not make seniority absolute. The employee can show that the actual system weakens the expectation on which the ordinary inference rests. Examples include an employer's frequent exercise of reserved amendment authority, sufficiently reducing expectations of adherence, or existing exceptions whose nature makes one further exception unlikely to matter. The examples are nonexclusive, and no numerical frequency threshold is prescribed. Bare reserved amendment authority does not automatically establish such circumstances; the employee must explain why an exception is reasonable in the actual setting.

The objection that this approach merges reasonableness with hardship overlooks their different subjects. The seniority inquiry concerns the character of the requested preference and employees' expectations; the employer's defense concerns significant difficulty or expense in its operations. The special-circumstances opportunity answers the reason for the ordinary seniority inference rather than requiring the employee to negate every operational burden. It remains a genuine evidentiary opportunity under ordinary summary-judgment principles, even for a bona fide system, not merely a chance to prove that the policy is a sham. Scalia and Thomas's contrary exclusion would foreclose that opportunity; Souter and Ginsburg's ordinary-inquiry approach would give seniority no comparable initial effect. The statute supports the qualified rule adopted here.

**Precedent treatment:** Hardison is used for the bounded seniority analogy; its Title VII setting and hardship threshold remain distinct. No prior ADA holding is overruled: this decision resolves the previously open seniority question under Title I.

### Remand applies the announced standard to the reviewed seniority claim

**Holding and operative rule:** The seniority-accommodation judgment is vacated and remanded so that the parties may seek summary judgment under the Court's standard, with Barnett able to establish special circumstances and US Airways able to assert its separate statutory hardship defense. Independent unreviewed accommodation grounds and the affirmed retaliation judgment retain their existing procedural status.

**Controlling explanation:** The lower courts applied different formulations: the District Court treated the system as establishing undue hardship, while the Court of Appeals placed seniority within a case-specific hardship inquiry. Neither applied the ordinary seniority inference together with the employee's opportunity to show special circumstances. The appropriate remedy is further consideration under the correct rule. Actual amendment practices, the character of exceptions and effects on employee expectations are application matters under that rule. The Court's limited seniority review supplies no basis to reopen the separate cargo, interactive-process or retaliation dispositions. A conflict with independently enforceable collective-bargaining obligations is outside the question presented by this noncontractual policy.

**Precedent treatment:** Toyota and Waffle House preserve the distinction between a statutory rule and proof of the remaining claim and remedy conditions; their separate coverage and enforcement holdings are unchanged.

- **Board of Trustees of the University of Alabama v. Garrett, February 21, 2001:** Preserves the distinct qualification, accommodation and demonstrated-hardship conditions; its state-employer enforcement holding supplies no seniority exemption.
- **Trans World Airlines, Inc. v. Hardison, 432 U.S. 63:** Supplies a limited analogy about seniority expectations. Its Title VII protection of seniority and more-than-de-minimis hardship threshold do not become the ADA's rule.
- **Sutton v. United Air Lines, Inc. and Murphy v. United Parcel Service, Inc., June 22, 1999; Toyota Motor Manufacturing, Kentucky, Inc. v. Williams, January 8, 2002:** Their actual-disability rules remain separate from qualification and accommodation; the seniority decision neither changes those rules nor adjudicates the disputed coverage-preservation matter.
- **EEOC v. Waffle House, Inc., January 15, 2002:** Its independent public-enforcement and remedy rules remain intact; permission to pursue statutory relief is distinct from establishing actual entitlement.
- **Olmstead v. L.C., June 22, 1999, and PGA TOUR, Inc. v. Martin, May 29, 2001:** Their respective Title II resource and Title III fundamental-alteration rules retain their own predicates. Neither replaces Title I's reasonable-accommodation and undue-hardship inquiries.

Under original ADA Title I, established seniority ordinarily makes a conflicting reassignment unreasonable, even under an employer-created system. The employee can establish special circumstances making an exception reasonable; that opportunity is substantive and nonexclusive. If reasonableness is established, the employer still bears the distinct significant-difficulty-or-expense defense. Neutrality alone does not immunize an employment policy, effectiveness alone does not establish reasonableness, and seniority is neither merely a hardship factor nor an absolute exemption. The rule takes effect April 29, 2002. Actual application and the remaining claim conditions proceed under these settled rules.

### City of Los Angeles v. Alameda Books, Inc. — 2002-05-13

The law permits this initial concentration inference while requiring the preserved classification, advancement, fit and expressive-access inquiries. The ordinance receives no final constitutional validation. Kennedy's more specific speech-preservation formulation remains his separate condition, and the dissent's present-record insufficiency conclusion does not become the Court's law.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK4_PUBLIC_LAW.md#city-of-los-angeles-v-alameda-books-merits-2002-05-13). [Record](../records/city_of_los_angeles_v_alameda_books_merits_2002-05-13.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The Ninth Circuit judgment is vacated, and the case is remanded for adjudication of the preserved classification, advancement, fit and expressive-access grounds consistently with the initial-inference holding. The courts must promptly reconsider existing injunctive relief under the governing standards. The ordinance is not finally upheld, and the vacatur alone supplies no automatic enforcement permission.

### Henderson v. Norris — 2002-05-13

Under the existing narrow proportionality rule, permanent imprisonment for this first conviction and materially equivalent tiny-sale conduct is unconstitutional. The conviction remains intact. The law establishes neither a general drug-weight schedule nor the replacement term. Pre-AEDPA independent legal review and Teague's separate finality requirement retain their distinct operation.

Exact controlling propositions, proposition-level authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK4_PUBLIC_LAW.md#henderson-v-norris-merits-2002-05-13). [Record](../records/henderson_v_norris_merits_2002-05-13.md).

**Current procedural stage:** The Eighth Circuit judgment is affirmed. Its implementing mandate begins Arkansas's ninety-day period for lawful resentencing under the conditional sentence-only writ, subject to ordinary mandate administration. The conviction remains in force. The Court specifies no maximum replacement term; lawful state sentencing must comply with the constitutional boundary announced here.

### Ashcroft v. American Civil Liberties Union; No. 00-1293; 535 U.S. 564. — 2002-05-13

Lower courts must evaluate COPA's actual statutory reach and adult-speech burden as a whole. The presence of community standards alone cannot sustain the reviewed categorical facial rationale, and the presence of other elements alone cannot establish ultimate validity. Neither a national standard nor categorical approval of local standards becomes controlling law. The existing adult-speech, substantial-overbreadth and justification rules remain operative. Full statutory validity, the factual adequacy of verification and the comparative effectiveness of alternative protections remain for the returned proceeding because the appellate rationale did not adjudicate them.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#ashcroft-v-aclu-merits-2002-05-13).

### Lapides v. Board of Regents of University System of Georgia; No. 01-298; 535 U.S. 613. — 2002-05-13

The open removal-waiver question is now resolved for state-law claims that a State allows against itself in its own courts: authorized voluntary removal relinquishes the federal-forum immunity objection despite an earlier express reservation. The substantive scope and manner of the State's waiver, valid causes of action and independent defenses continue to govern liability. Ford's inconsistent internal-authorization rationale cannot defeat this consequence. Removal of claims for which state-court immunity remains intact and an otherwise valid federal claim are outside this decision's confined question. Existing rules of statutory defendant status, original jurisdiction and discretionary supplemental jurisdiction remain separate and operative.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#lapides-v-board-of-regents-merits-2002-05-13).

### Verizon Communications Inc. v. FCC; Nos. 00-511, 00-555, 00-587, 00-590 and 00-602; 535 U.S. 467. — 2002-05-13

Forward-looking pricing is legally available, and this efficient-technology/existing-wire-center methodology survives the facial statutory and constitutional objections with its actual cost and procedural limits. The Constitution does not require historical-cost reimbursement on this showing. Sections 51.315(c)–(f) are restored as necessary assistance for a requester not reasonably able to combine, subject to lawful element entitlement, separate paragraph conditions, supported network objections and lawful compensation. The Court supplies neither a new element list nor a carrier-specific rate or connection result. State commissions retain their assigned application functions; supported statutory and constitutional challenges to particular outcomes remain available.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#verizon-communications-v-fcc-merits-2002-05-13).

### Voting Integrity Project, Inc. v. Keisling; reviewed judgment 259 F.3d 1169 (9th Cir. 2001); no Supreme Court docket supplied. — 2002-05-13

The federal election-day provisions permit advance casting within an election that stays open through the prescribed day and is legally completed then, including Oregon's universal mail procedure and materially equivalent advance-ballot features with receipt required by that day. Congressional selection and presidential-elector appointment each receive a direct statutory holding. An earlier legally final winner cannot be saved by postponing announcement or certification. This decision changes no receipt deadline, supplies no permission for ballots first received afterward, and establishes no independent constitutional entitlement to a particular voting method.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#voting-integrity-project-v-keisling-merits-2002-05-13).

### Alabama v. Shelton; No. 00-1214; 535 U.S. 654. — 2002-05-20

Courts may retain a valid uncounseled conviction and lawful noncustodial punishment, but may not use it to support a suspended imprisonment sentence without the required counsel or valid waiver at the guilt adjudication. Ordinary revocation counsel does not cure that defect. The separate full-counseled-retrial arrangement is not foreclosed; lesser safeguards and freestanding probation retain the precise limits stated above. Nichols's narrow later-use permission and the independent actual-waiver requirement remain in force.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#alabama-v-shelton-merits-2002-05-20).

### Brown v. Gilmore; reviewed judgment 258 F.3d 265 (4th Cir. 2001); no Supreme Court docket supplied. — 2002-05-20

Genuine school silence leaving thought private is constitutionally permissible under the stated conditions. Religious supporters, an express permission to pray and compulsory silence do not themselves establish official worship. Courts must still examine actual governmental function, attribution and substantial pressure in the school setting. Neither secular objectives nor neutral statutory wording protects actual directed devotion. The decision sustains this facial arrangement without restoring independent Lemon prongs or establishing immunity for later administration.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#brown-v-gilmore-merits-2002-05-20).

### Mathias v. WorldCom Technologies, Inc.; No. 00-878; 535 U.S. 682. — 2002-05-20

The writ dismissal creates no telecommunications, consent, officer-relief or compensation holding. The Court's explanation is confined to this vehicle; existing doctrines governing adverse collateral adjudications and continuing appellate stakes remain intact. The lower judgment remains effective without this Court's endorsement of its reasoning.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#mathias-v-worldcom-technologies-dig-2002-05-20).

### United States v. Cotton; No. 01-687; 535 U.S. 625. — 2002-05-20

An omitted aggravating quantity is a real constitutional charging and jury error but does not withdraw §3231 jurisdiction over this federal prosecution. Forfeited claims remain subject to Rule 52(b)'s distinct requirements. This threshold-specific fourth-condition decision leaves the sentences undisturbed without authorizing future element omissions or converting judicial quantity findings into a verdict. Preserved error and materially different contested records retain their own governing analysis.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-v-cotton-merits-2002-05-20).

### Verizon Maryland Inc. v. Public Service Commission of Maryland; United States v. Public Service Commission of Maryland; Nos. 00-1531 and 00-1711; 535 U.S. 635. — 2002-05-20

Section 1331 remains available for the substantial federal enforcement challenge even on the assumption that §252(e)(6)'s determination-review sentence does not apply. Its separate failure-to-act exclusivity remains intact. Responsible commissioners may face prospective restraint of ongoing enforcement alleged to violate federal law, without treating private financial effects alone as State damages or the Commission's absence as an automatic bar. Full determination-review coverage, direct Commission amenability, consent and the private statutory cause remain undecided because this limited review does not require them. Federal compliance, compensation merits and the availability and scope of the actual requested remedies require ordinary adjudication below under the stated rules; establishing the forum does not decide those matters.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#verizon-maryland-v-public-service-commission-merits-2002-05-20).

### Bell v. Cone; No. 01-400; 535 U.S. 685. — 2002-05-28

Actual sentencing advocacy prevents treating these particular omissions as complete adversarial abandonment. Ordinary tactical choices remain reviewable under Strickland and the applicable §2254 standard; a strategy label is not conclusive. The independently reasonable state-performance adjudication defeats this confined claim despite the district court's added prejudice error. Genuine development allegations, their procedural treatment and other unreviewed sentence grounds remain distinct.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#Bell-v-Cone-Merits-2002-05-28).

### Federal Maritime Commission v. South Carolina State Ports Authority; No. 01-46; 535 U.S. 743. — 2002-05-28

A state-operated port cannot demand dismissal of this Shipping Act administrative complaint merely because a private complainant initiated it and seeks reparations. Both monetary and prospective requests may receive statutory adjudication; the agency's forum does not itself decide coverage, violation or relief. This is a Commission-process rule, not universal commercial waiver, a new private judicial cause of action or a ruling on later collection immunity.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#Federal-Maritime-Commission-v-South-Carolina-State-Ports-Authority-Merits-2002-05-28).

### Festo Corp. v. Shoketsu Kinzoku Kogyo Kabushiki Co.; No. 00-1543; 535 U.S. 722. — 2002-05-28

Narrowing for any patentability requirement, including disclosure, may trigger estoppel. The law now expressly presumes surrender of the asserted equivalent after that trigger, while permitting concrete objective rebuttal directed to what the amendment reasonably relinquished. Required-narrowing explanation and scope rebuttal are separate burdens. Every material limitation remains operative; neither rejection of a complete bar nor successful estoppel rebuttal establishes infringement. Older records receive fair treatment without automatic evidentiary reopening. The three disputed amendments meet the trigger; only particular-equivalent scope rebuttal and the surviving ordinary procedural and infringement applications return for decision.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#Festo-Corp-v-Shoketsu-Kinzoku-Kogyo-Kabushiki-Co-Merits-2002-05-28).

Courts reviewing claimant-funded fees for successful court representation must begin with a lawful contingent agreement and independently determine a reasonable award within the statutory ceiling. The contract percentage is not presumptively due; supported reductions, time-and-rate evidence and an explained individual decision protect benefits. Neither exclusive lodestar replacement nor automatic maximum payment is the governing rule. Administrative compensation, EAJA eligibility and payment, and the statutory certification and assessment conditions remain separate.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#Gisbrecht-v-Barnhart-Merits-2002-05-28).

A settlement court with enforcement jurisdiction must separately establish an Anti-Injunction Act exception for the particular restraint. Policy-specific exclusions retain practical effect: shared facts alone do not convert a preserved claim into a released one. Supported protection against actual released relief or inconsistent adjudication remains available. Ordinary reopening, admissibility, privilege and claim-specific determinations remain with the appropriate court under their governing rules.

Title VI's direct prospective action and §1983's individual-right inquiry remain distinct. This permitting claim may proceed under the established direct route upon the actual regulatory and defendant predicates, while the asserted regulation-created §1983 effects right remains unavailable. Section 601's intentional-discrimination route retains its independent requirement. The legal route is settled; coverage, violation, causation and relief require adjudication on the record.

Holdings, authority, qualifications, precedent treatment and remedy: [exact public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md#South-Camden-Citizens-in-Action-v-New-Jersey-Department-of-Environmental-Protection-Merits-2002-05-28).

### Compaq Computer Corp. v. Commissioner — 2002-05-28

#### I. Correct tax characterization requires consistent profit measurement

**Holding and operative rule:** A court independently reviews a transaction's legal characterization for federal tax purposes while reviewing its subsidiary historical findings for clear error. Where the taxpayer owns the dividend entitlement and bears the foreign tax liability, the pretax calculation includes the gross dividend, including withholding paid on the taxpayer's behalf; a worldwide after-tax calculation must also account for the corresponding legally available United States foreign tax credit.

**Authority:** Part I of Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies nine votes for this rule and its application.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that the tax paid on Compaq's behalf remains part of its gross dividend. Old Colony Trust v. Commissioner treats discharge of the taxpayer's obligation by another as income to the taxpayer; Diedrich v. Commissioner applies the same principle when a recipient pays the transferor's tax. Treasury Regulation §1.901-2(f)(1) identifies the foreign-tax payer by legal liability even when a withholding agent remits payment, and §1.901-2(f)(2)(i) preserves that treatment when another party assumes the liability. Compaq held the dividend entitlement on the record date and bore the Netherlands tax obligation. The withholding therefore did not turn the gross dividend into fictional income.

The Tax Court's calculation subtracted the foreign tax but excluded the corresponding United States credit, yielding an apparent loss of approximately $1.5 million. That calculation measures neither pretax nor worldwide after-tax return. Correctly including the gross dividend and actual transaction charges produces approximately $1.894 million before tax; accounting for the credit and approximately $644,000 in net worldwide tax produces approximately $1.25 million after tax. The Court rejects the Commissioner's inconsistent measure, not every inquiry into a transaction's actual economic operation.

Frank Lyon v. United States separates reviewable legal tax characterization from subsidiary historical facts. The findings about the one-hour meeting, limited investigation, promoter coordination and brief holding period remain intact. Correcting the legal computation does not find that Compaq conducted extensive diligence, and the profit figures do not establish a universal minimum-profit test.

**Precedent treatment:** Old Colony Trust and Diedrich are applied to withholding that discharges the dividend owner's tax liability; Frank Lyon's distinction between historical facts and legal tax characterization is applied without change.

#### II. This actual market transaction has economic substance and sufficient business purpose

**Holding and operative rule:** The established purchase of Royal Dutch American Depository Receipts, acquisition of the dividend entitlement and resale must be recognized under the applicable income, capital-loss and foreign-tax-credit provisions: its actual return, acquired economic rights and market exposure establish economic substance and sufficient business purpose despite its tax objective. Substantive-sham scrutiny remains available, but tax motivation, rapid resale and the reduction of avoidable risk do not by themselves defeat recognition of this demonstrated arrangement.

**Authority:** Part II of Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies nine votes for the complete bounded holding.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that these actual economic interests distinguish the arrangement from a tax form without qualifying substance. Gregory v. Helvering requires statutory substance rather than labels; Knetsch v. United States rejects purported borrowing without genuine economic function. Frank Lyon requires recognition of genuine multiparty rights and duties supported by substantive business considerations even when favorable tax treatment helps explain the transaction.

Compaq acquired and disposed of securities through 46 transactions on the New York Stock Exchange, held the dividend entitlement on the record date, and obtained an actual pretax return after transaction charges. Prices could and did move; execution and dividend risks remained. The promoter selected the counterparty, trade sizes and prices, and management undertook only limited investigation. Those facts support the Commissioner's contention that tax consequences drove the arrangement. They do not erase its established return and economic position or transform the trades into a closed market whose prices Compaq controlled. Neither nominal title nor reporting income alone supplies the conclusion.

Winn-Dixie Stores v. Commissioner preserves substantive qualification even when a taxpayer avoids a particular statutory prohibition. Its accepted absence of both nontax business purpose and pretax profit potential differs from this record. The credit scheme does not displace that scrutiny, but scrutiny must give effect to the actual economic rights and legally correct computation. The established facts resolve substance and purpose here; a new purpose trial would identify no remaining material fact necessary to this disposition.

The Court adopts no safe harbor for nominal profits, fabricated trades, circular controlled pricing, nonexistent foreign tax liability or failure of an independent credit limitation. The case presents both substance and sufficient purpose, so it does not require a universal choice among conjunctive, disjunctive or factor-based formulations for records lacking one of those features. The 1997 §901(k) holding-period provision does not govern the 1992 transaction or establish automatic validity for all earlier transactions.

**Precedent treatment:** Gregory and Knetsch retain substantive-sham scrutiny; Frank Lyon is applied to the real economic rights acquired here; Winn-Dixie's distinction between avoiding a particular restriction and substantively qualifying for a tax benefit remains intact.

#### III. Recognition removes the reviewed adjustment and its dependent penalty

**Holding and operative rule:** Within the Commissioner's stipulation that the transaction's reporting is otherwise unchallenged, recognition removes the deficiency and §6662 negligence penalty insofar as they depend on the rejected substantive-sham adjustment, leaving only computation under the applicable statutory requirements. The contingent request to deduct transaction expenses if the transaction is disregarded has no operative work once the transaction is recognized.

**Authority:** Part III of Stevens's opinion of the Court has nine votes for the deficiency, penalty and bounded computation treatment: Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. Its concluding treatment of the contingent expense-deduction request has eight opinion votes: Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; Stone-Zsela agrees with that disposition only.

**Controlling explanation:** Recognition restores the challenged income, loss and foreign-credit treatment within the reporting dispute the Commissioner has preserved. The sham adjustment therefore supplies no resulting underpayment on which to sustain the dependent negligence penalty. That conclusion does not treat limited investigation as invariably reasonable care. The alternative expense request was conditioned on disregard; recognition removes that condition and makes the alternative unnecessary. Further proceedings implement the applicable statutory computation within the stipulation, rather than reopen the case to search for an unasserted disallowance theory. Independent ownership, reporting and credit-limit objections excluded by the stipulation remain outside the question reviewed, and their statutory requirements remain in force.

**Precedent treatment:** The remedy implements the recognition required by Frank Lyon and the substantive distinction preserved in Winn-Dixie; it adds no separate rule of immunity from negligence penalties or credit limitations.

- Gregory v. Helvering, 293 U.S. 465: applied to retain the requirement of statutory substance; it does not make tax motivation alone disqualifying.
- Knetsch v. United States, 364 U.S. 361: distinguished because this transaction acquired genuine market rights and an actual return; its treatment of economically empty borrowing remains in force.
- Frank Lyon Co. v. United States, 435 U.S. 561, 580–584 & n.16: applied to genuine economic rights despite tax planning and to independent review of legal tax characterization with deference to subsidiary historical findings.
- Old Colony Trust Co. v. Commissioner, 279 U.S. 716, 729: applied because withholding discharges Compaq's own tax liability and therefore forms part of its income.
- Diedrich v. Commissioner, 457 U.S. 191, 199–200: applied for the same liability-discharge principle; payment by another does not remove the economic benefit from the taxpayer's income.
- Winn-Dixie Stores, Inc. v. Commissioner, decided February 20, 2002: distinguished on its accepted absence of both economic predicates; its preservation of substance scrutiny, enacted tax consequences and transaction-specific assessment remains controlling.

From May 28, 2002, profit analysis of a dividend transaction with established ownership and foreign tax liability must treat the gross dividend and the legally available credit consistently. This demonstrated market arrangement satisfies substance and purpose, and its reviewed sham-dependent adjustment and penalty cannot stand. The decision adds that application to the existing substance doctrine without replacing it with a nominal-profit threshold, an ownership-only safe harbor or a universal test for cases with different economic findings. Independent statutory qualification requirements retain their force. The 1997 holding-period rule supplies no governing standard for this 1992 transaction.

Exact public holding, authority, treatment and remedy: [public law](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK6_PUBLIC_LAW.md#compaq-computer-corp-v-commissioner-merits-2002-05-28).

### Holmes Group, Inc. v. Vornado Air Circulation Systems, Inc. — 2002-06-03

#### I. A patent counterclaim does not supply the incorporated arising-under basis

**Holding and operative rule:** Under the then-operative 28 U.S.C. §§1295(a)(1) and 1338(a), a patent counterclaim alone, including a compulsory counterclaim, does not make an action arise under patent law when the plaintiff's well-pleaded complaint supplies no patent-law basis. The Federal Circuit therefore lacks the asserted specialized jurisdiction over this nonpatent Rule 54(b) judgment; that conclusion does not eliminate the district court's independent jurisdiction over Holmes's federal trade-dress action.

**Authority:** Part I of Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer, commands seven votes for the rule and application. O'Connor and Ginsburg agree with jurisdictional vacatur on the distinct ground described in their separate writing.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that Congress made the plaintiff's well-pleaded claim the relevant source of patent jurisdiction. Christianson v. Colt identifies the incorporated §1338 basis: patent law must create the claim or supply a substantial question necessarily resolved to obtain the requested relief. Section 1295 refers to that jurisdictional source; its provision for jurisdiction based in whole or in part on §1338 permits qualifying mixed actions without changing the source of the patent claim. The express copyright, mask-work and trademark-only exception preserves regional review where no other §1338(a) claim supplies the specialized basis.

A counterclaim is an affirmative claim, not merely a defense. Cardinal Chemical v. Morton recognizes its independent controversy, but does not decide whether that claim can itself create the specialized appellate allocation over a nonpatent complaint. Extending Christianson's source-of-claim reasoning answers that distinct question. Rule 13's compulsory-claim duty and Rule 54(b)'s partial-final-judgment mechanism do not rewrite the jurisdictional statutes. The coercive action corresponding to Holmes's declaratory trade-dress claim concerns trade dress; a different patent action Vornado could bring does not transform that complaint.

Uniform patent adjudication supports Vornado's competing interpretation, but cannot replace the statutory reference to the well-pleaded claim. The Court rejects that interpretation without requiring a finding that the counterclaim is frivolous or manipulative. The patent counterclaim remains real and pending, yet does not confer this jurisdiction. The rule addresses a counterclaim-only patent basis. No actual or constructive amendment adding a patent claim to Holmes's complaint is presented, and the Court decides no jurisdictional consequence of such an amendment or universal rule fixing appellate jurisdiction forever at the initial filing. Patent infringement, functionality and collateral-estoppel merits remain outside this jurisdictional review.

**Precedent treatment:** Christianson's incorporated source-of-claim analysis is extended to the compulsory-counterclaim question; Cardinal Chemical's independent-counterclaim rule remains intact but supplies no new appellate grant.

#### II. The appeal must be transferred to the Tenth Circuit

**Holding and operative rule:** Section 1631 requires a court lacking jurisdiction to transfer an appeal when the receiving court could have heard it when originally noticed and transfer serves the interest of justice; the appeal then proceeds as though noticed in that court on its original notice date. Those conditions are satisfied here, so the Federal Circuit's judgment is vacated and that court must transfer the appeal to the Tenth Circuit.

**Authority:** Part II of Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies nine votes for this rule, its application and the definite transfer mandate.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court holds that transfer preserves this timely appeal in the court authorized to hear it. The district court entered final judgment on the trade-dress claim under Rule 54(b), expressly finding no just reason for delay. Holmes acknowledges that Vornado timely noticed the appeal.
