Earlier alibi descriptions and earlier attendance did not necessarily establish what the witnesses would then say, whether they could be obtained within a reasonable time, or the current diligence and other facts needed to evaluate the delay. A focused contemporaneous presentation also creates a reviewable record. A known procedural ground may support appellate affirmance even when the trial judge relied on scheduling; an uncited requirement is not thereby useless. Osborne involved repetition of a legal objection already foreclosed, not the initial support for a factual request. The dissent objects that searching earlier portions of the record for substantial compliance can displace the State's actual presentation rule. It does not hold that every formally regular rule is adequate in every application, and relies on no finding about why the witnesses left or whether their alibi was true.

These published positions are noncontrolling at the stated scope. [Public opinion account](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#lee-v-kemna-merits-2002-01-22).

### United States v. Microsoft Corp. — 2002-01-22

Stevens concurs in Parts II and III and dissents from Part I's uniform direction. Jefferson Parish and Kodak preserve the established category when independent demand and a separable forced tie satisfy its elements. Genuine functional integration may warrant fuller competitive inquiry, but useful distribution of interfaces does not make compulsory retention of a complete browser an innovative design. His California Dental position likewise requires examination of the actual restraint. He would distinguish contractual and removal restraints that fit the conventional category from integrated functionality; he would neither reinstate the entire undifferentiated trial judgment nor decide ultimate tying liability.

O'Connor, joined by Scalia, Thomas and Breyer, concurs in Parts I and II and dissents from Part III. Under all three Liljeberg interests, they find the existing appellate allocation sufficient. The plaintiffs did not participate in the misconduct; repeating completed adjudication can impose serious costs; appellate review differentiated sustainable liability from rejected theories; and reassignment plus replacement of the entire remedy materially protect deterrence and public confidence. They expressly account for interviews beginning before the findings. The misconduct's pronounced remedial expression supports their line without making all earlier contacts irrelevant. They adopt neither an actual-bias prerequisite nor an automatic rule that clear-error review cures disqualification. Their disagreement concerns the sufficiency of this particular allocation, not the governing appearance standard or a belief that the appellate court conducted a new trial.

These published positions are noncontrolling at the stated scope. [Public opinion account](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#united-states-v-microsoft-corp-merits-2002-01-22).

### Barnhart v. Sigmon Coal Co. — 2002-02-19

**Stevens, joined by O'Connor and Breyer, dissenting.** The dissent would reverse and remand for application of an inclusive succession rule. Congress allocated existing health-benefit obligations across continuing business relationships. Reading the successor sentence in that integrated structure avoids the anomaly of reaching a related person's successor while exempting the closer direct successor that continued the coal business and assumed its agreement. The contextual treatment of successors in 1 U.S.C. sections 1 and 5, together with the labor-law succession principles illustrated by Wiley, Golden State Bottling and Fall River Dyeing, supports that reading; those authorities do not establish liability for every solvent purchaser.

The dissent recognizes that a differentiated list and a separate prospective transfer provision could reflect compromise. Its answer is that the entire succession structure supplies the context for that list, and the Court's separation defeats the allocation Congress made. Legislative explanations corroborate that construction rather than replacing the text. The dissent does not treat a bargaining discount as a proved fact, impose unrestricted federal common-law liability, or decide the constitutional question. Preserved successor-status and other application issues remain for the proper tribunal on remand.

These published positions are noncontrolling at the stated scope. [Public opinion account](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#barnhart-v-sigmon-coal-co-merits-2002-02-19).

### Romeu v. Cohen — 2002-02-19

**O'Connor, joined by Scalia, Thomas and Breyer, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** The four would affirm both equality dismissals. Residence ordinarily identifies membership in a State's electorate. Congress and New York may accommodate former residents outside the domestic political system without extending the accommodation to people who establish membership in another domestic polity. Burdick, Timmons and Schrader permit meaningful electoral categories and require identification of the relevant electorate before the severity of a lost opportunity determines scrutiny. Saenz concerns equal citizenship in the new State, not a continuing former-state vote.

The dissent acknowledges that Puerto Rico's local elections and Resident Commissioner do not replace presidential participation. That absence makes the exclusion consequential but does not establish continuing membership in New York's electorate. Domestic political membership and incremental overseas accommodation provide a rational basis for the distinction, without making territorial power unreviewable or every residence rule valid. UOCAVA is a federal floor, so New York could extend eligibility; that permission does not establish a constitutional duty to do so. The proposed decree's precision does not cure the majority's antecedent membership error. The four join the prospective-review and federal-floor conclusions and the rejection of independent permanent-membership, travel, Article IV and general due-process entitlements.

Stone-Zsela joins the equality rules and prospective relief but does not reach the independent entitlement theories rejected by the eight Associates. That join limit supplies no contrary entitlement rule.

These published positions are noncontrolling at the stated scope. [Public opinion account](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#romeu-v-cohen-merits-2002-02-19).

### Wisconsin Department of Health and Family Services v. Blumer — 2002-02-20

**Stevens, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor and Scalia, dissenting.** The dissent would affirm the resources-first requirement and return the matter for a calculation using supported income and resource figures. Section (e)(2)(C) directs an increased resource allowance when the community spouse's protected income is inadequate. Predicting income from resources legally attributable to that spouse differs from predicting another person's optional payment. Section (d)(1)(B)'s posteligibility transfer does not add a prerequisite that the spouse depend on that payment before receiving the resource protection; denying eligibility can prevent the projected arrangement from beginning.

The dissent recognizes that (b)(1) is not a reciprocal nonattribution rule and that (b)(2) expressly addresses posteligibility attribution. Its point is the scheme's separation of ownership, resource protection and voluntary support, not an invented symmetric prohibition. The Court's answer that every method forecasts future circumstances fails to explain why Congress's resource protection may depend first on another person's voluntary transfer. A future payment might not occur or might diminish; those are objections to the legal method, not findings about Irene's later conduct. Administrative flexibility, conservation of funds and expertise cannot supply the missing compulsory transfer or displace the specific statutory command. The agency's changed view is not invalid merely because it changed, but its reasoning must answer this objection. The dissent asserts no constitutional right to preserve assets and would award no unsupported dollar amount.

These published positions are noncontrolling at the stated scope. [Public opinion account](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK2_PUBLIC_LAW.md#wisconsin-department-of-health-and-family-services-v-blumer-merits-2002-02-20).

### Porter v. Nussle — 2002-02-26

[Record](../records/porter-v-nussle_merits_2002-02-26.md)

No separate writing is filed.

### Swierkiewicz v. Sorema N.A. — 2002-02-26

[Record](../records/swierkiewicz-v-sorema-na_merits_2002-02-26.md)

No separate writing is filed.

### Guest v. Leis — 2002-02-27

[Record](../records/guest-v-leis_merits_2002-02-27.md)

**O'Connor, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** Confidential access credentials have an exclusion function materially different from ordinary provider records. She would vacate the categorical dismissal only at that scope and require a claimant-specific showing on remand. Names, addresses and other routine identifying information deliberately conveyed for administration ordinarily fall within Miller and Smith; expected confidentiality alone does not distinguish them. Ferguson and Denius require attention to what was entrusted and the actual use, but do not establish the broader account rule adopted by five Justices. She joins objective personal immunity and rejects a requirement of actual authoritative reliance.

**Scalia, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** The three would affirm the subscriber-information judgment on the merits as well as on personal immunity. Miller and Smith do not depend on public dissemination: they address information voluntarily conveyed to a provider, often with an expectation of limited use. Calling the records digital or credential-related does not establish a constitutional distinction from that settled rule. Kyllo's protection of concealed home information does not control operational information held by a different person. Each claimant still must establish a personal interest and an intrusion; the existing equipment and private-message dispositions are distinct. The three join Part II's independent objective immunity ground.

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** The Chief Justice joins the full privacy rule and both institutional remands but would also vacate personal immunity. After an actionable violation is established under the complete substantive rule, including its reasonable-factual-mistake allowances, he would require the official to identify binding precedent or enacted authority directly authorizing the challenged conduct, prove genuine reliance and show reasonable grounds for treating the permission as valid and applicable. An instruction accurately transmitting the authority may prove reliance; silence, unsettled law, departmental practice, general investigative authority or an ungrounded supervisor or prosecutor assurance would not. Knowing material falsification, concealed disqualifying facts or manufactured authority would defeat reasonable reliance. So would disregard of the authority's apparent limits, apparent unlawfulness under controlling constitutional principles, or a factual mistake already rejected under the substantive merits rule. The protection would include an unexpected change in controlling law that the official could not reasonably anticipate.

He would overrule Harlow and Anderson only insofar as insufficiently clear law independently excuses a proven violation, disapprove Wilson to that extent and retain Elder's consideration of relevant law for the new inquiry. Section 1983's remedial commitment, in his view, warrants that change while concrete reliance protects legitimate official decisiveness. He acknowledges an additional factual inquiry and litigation cost; ordinary pleading and summary-judgment procedures would remain. A valid warrant may defeat the constitutional claim, but its existence alone does not prove actual reliance, and later litigation citations cannot establish the official's 1995 knowledge. A prior judgment granting personal immunity would not itself constitute authority permitting the underlying conduct. He would allow the officials to make the newly required showing on remand. His proposal would not reopen final judgments, change the plaintiff's other elements, create an institutional remedy, displace an independent statutory defense or decide a separate absolute immunity.

### Raygor v. Regents of University of Minnesota — 2002-02-27

[Record](../records/raygor-v-regents-of-university-of-minnesota_merits_2002-02-27.md)

**Stevens, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Breyer, dissenting.** Section 1367(d) protects a related claim actually asserted alongside an authorized federal controversy even when immunity prevents a federal merits judgment on that claim. The filing-related word asserted does work distinct from maintainable. The pending-period and thirty-day protection, subject to longer state tolling, preserve an otherwise available state forum rather than create a new cause, impose liability or waive immunity. The subsection (a) reference supplies original-jurisdiction and relatedness predicates; it need not make eventual adjudicability a further condition absent from the dismissal protection. Pennhurst therefore survives this reading. Kimel supplies the valid federal ADEA anchor, while Schacht keeps the supplemental defense claim-specific. Vermont Agency's exclusion from a sovereign-defendant action does not answer this different procedural effect. The dissent would reverse the categorical exclusion, leaving actual filing arithmetic and remaining independent defenses for the state court and leaving the federal judgment closed.

**Stone-Zsela, separately dissenting.** Congress's authority to implement Article III, together with the Necessary and Proper Clause, permits this reasonable protection against forfeiture while a federal tribunal determines its authority over related claims. The clear statement needed to authorize a treasury judgment cannot be transferred without analysis to a rule preserving time in a state forum the State otherwise permits. Text and procedural structure support coverage: immunity dismissal does not erase the fact that the claim was asserted with a valid federal controversy. The Chief Justice would apply the enacted pending-period and thirty-day protection, subject to a longer state period, while preserving the State's distinct forum immunity, substantive defenses and any independent finality defense. His position supplies no new state cause of action, no authority to reopen the federal dismissal and no automatic finding that these state filings were timely. His proposed construction and remand do not command a majority.

### Delaney v. DeTella — 2002-03-04

### New York v. FERC / Enron Power Marketing, Inc. v. FERC — 2002-03-04

[Record](../records/new-york-v-ferc-enron-power-marketing-inc-v-ferc_merits_2002-03-04.md)

**Thomas, joined by Scalia and Kennedy, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** The three join the unbundled-transmission holding and affirmance in No. 00-568. They would vacate the Enron component and require FERC to reconsider under the proper statutory understanding. Interstate transmission does not cease to be transmission merely because a utility sells it with energy for one charge. Section 206's mandatory corrective duty must be assessed without a categorical jurisdictional exclusion. The agency's coexisting unnecessary-remedy statement is real, but insufficiently explained and independent of its contrary account of jurisdiction to justify the refusal. MCI requires compliance with actual statutory obligations; Gulf Power keeps coverage and an adequately reasoned regulatory choice separate; the reasoning requirements preserved in Mead cannot be satisfied by probable power or counsel's substitute explanation. The three would require an agency determination of the relevant discrimination and an appropriate lawful response. They do not find every bundled transaction unlawful, relieve Enron of its burden, or direct immediate adoption of the open-access tariff. Existing wholesale and unbundled-retail requirements would remain while the agency supplied the necessary explanation.

### Ruiz v. United States (Ruiz v. Johnson) — 2002-03-04

[Record](../records/ruiz-v-united-states-ruiz-v-johnson_merits_2002-03-04.md)

No separate writing is filed.

### United States v. Vonn — 2002-03-04

[Record](../records/united-states-v-vonn_merits_2002-03-04.md)

**Stevens, concurring in part and dissenting in part.** Stevens joins Part II's use of relevant material throughout the official record and agrees that the appellate judgment should be vacated. He rejects Parts I and III's transfer of the effect burden to the defendant. Rule 11 assigns the judge affirmative duties during a procedure in which an uninformed defendant may not recognize the missing advice. Its specific harmless-error provision should therefore leave the Government responsible for showing that the omission was harmless. McCarthy supplies the distinctive judicial obligation; Stevens's Olano dissent distinguishes procedural protections from easily measured outcome injury, and his Johnson join did not decide whether Rule 11(h) controls this separate plea procedure. He would remand for whole-record harmless-error review under Rule 11(h). Earlier recorded advisements may establish actual knowledge under that inquiry, but representation is not itself compliance, and neither automatic invalidation nor invented off-record advice follows from his position.

### Young v. United States — 2002-03-04

### Edelman v. Lynchburg College — 2002-03-19

[Record](../records/edelman-v-lynchburg-college_merits_2002-03-19.md)

**Thomas, concurring.** He joins the Court on the understanding that section 713(a) expressly delegates procedural rulemaking, this regulation is reasonable, Title VII does not prohibit it and its promulgation complies with the Administrative Procedure Act. All four conditions matter. The rule completes verification of an existing timely charge; it neither waives a statutory deadline nor recognizes an unrestricted equitable power to excuse procedural requirements. His qualification accompanies his full join in the Court's explanation, rather than substituting a judgment-only ground.

**O'Connor, joined by Scalia, concurring in the judgment.** Reading the provisions together most naturally calls for a verified charge within the filing period. But the statute does not expressly define charge in a way that unambiguously compels that reading. Section 713(a)'s procedural delegation therefore permits the EEOC's reasonable alternative, which preserves both timely initiation and later verified accountability. The rule's binding operation and notice-and-comment repromulgation in 1977 distinguish it from the revisable, nonbinding tariff letters addressed in Mead. Commercial Office Products supports deference on this technical agency procedure. The concurrence also questions how recognition of a possible future reasonable agency change can be reconciled with wholly reserving the deference issue. Neither Justice joins the assertion that the current rule is the best independent statutory reading. Both preserve actual verification, the employer's protection before a required merits response and the separate inquiry into whether the initial letter was a charge.

### Milstein v. Cooley — 2002-03-19

[Record](../records/milstein-v-cooley_merits_2002-03-19.md)

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela concurs in part and dissents in part; no other Justice joins. He agrees that investigative fabrication, personal accusation, detective work and press statements lack absolute immunity. He also preserves immunity for the counsel motion and ordinary advocacy outside his proposed exception. He would vacate the remaining dismissal insofar as it categorically protects knowing material factual fabrication or deliberate false presentation causing an actionable deprivation.

His exception requires knowing fabrication of a materially false fact, deliberate material falsification of an evidentiary record, or knowing presentation of such false facts; an independently actionable constitutional deprivation; the official's own responsibility; and the governing causal connection. Materiality concerns the fact's significance in authorizing or sustaining the deprivation. Error, disputed inference, adverse credibility, mistaken testimony, erroneous legal judgment, malicious motive, reversal of conviction and an unfavorable prosecution outcome do not suffice. The claimant retains ordinary proof burdens, and loss of immunity proves none of these elements.

He would partially overrule Imbler and Fletcher, and Burns to the same extent, because the categorical advocacy bar defeats redress for deliberate manufacture of the factual premise constitutional process must test. He acknowledges Imbler's common-law account and the especially strong continuity owed a statutory precedent. He does not claim that historical immunity already contained the exception or that congressional silence enacted it.

The strongest opposing interest belongs to honest prosecutors: easy accusations of deliberate falsity may be costly to disprove and chill independent advocacy. Criminal sanctions and discipline also supply real deterrence. Stone-Zsela accepts that his proposal increases discovery and litigation costs and weakens protection from suit; saying that no one may legitimately falsify evidence does not answer those costs. At this defined boundary, he gives greater weight to adjudicating a deliberately manufactured constitutional injury. Institutional planning around immunity is a real reliance interest, but no property title, completed transaction or final judgment must be undone. The change would govern this and future open cases without reopening final judgments.

Crawford-El permits clarification, dispositive legal rulings, sequenced relevant discovery and ordinary summary judgment. It does not permit a new heightened evidentiary quantum or a requirement to prove knowledge before obtaining discovery necessary to prove it. These safeguards reduce costs without eliminating them. The claim remains act-specific, not a general dishonesty tort or entitlement to sue for every malicious prosecution. The counsel motion does not become actionable merely because the prosecutors allegedly wanted a conviction.

He would remand for that bounded inquiry. Qualified immunity remains reserved and must be decided under the law actually governing it, including applicable intervening decisions; his proposed absolute-immunity change neither grants nor rejects that defense. Constitutional elements, personal participation, materiality, causation, damages and remaining defenses require independent adjudication. No separate witness immunity, independent constitutional privilege, municipal-liability rule, new interlocutory appeal or mandatory discovery or trial program is resolved.

### Ragsdale v. Wolverine World Wide, Inc. — 2002-03-19

[Record](../records/ragsdale-v-wolverine-world-wide-inc_merits_2002-03-19.md)

**O'Connor, joined by Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, dissenting.** The FMLA does not give an employer an unconditional right to count all qualifying company leave retroactively against the protected allotment. Section 2654 permits the Secretary to require timely designation as a condition on that counting. The rule identifies which leave consumes the statutory right, rather than creating a monetary award for every omission. Greater-benefit provisions and the need for usable notice support keeping undesignated company leave distinct from the statutory allotment.

The rule's safeguards matter. Designation follows qualifying information; the ordinary two-business-day period permits extenuating circumstances. Later knowledge and provisional designation pending certification can permit the specified retrospective operation. The dissent does not treat every first-day failure as irreversible or require all company benefits to run sequentially. Nor does it assume that Ragsdale was eligible when she first stopped work in February; the supported eligibility date is the March extension.

The majority's lack-of-prejudice objection, in the dissent's view, confuses the entitlement's lawful definition with the requirements for an actual award. A predictable designation rule makes leave usable without forcing an employee to reconstruct every hypothetical choice. Mourning supports appropriate prophylactic implementation, and Christensen's distinction between an advisory position and a valid regulation leaves this delegated rule open for independent evaluation. MCI's explicit filing duty and Sigmon Coal's missing liable-person category differ from the absence of an unconditional concurrent-counting command here.

The four would reverse and remand to apply the undiminished entitlement and then determine the actual violation and appropriate relief under section 2617. They do not infer earlier work capacity, a medically available reduced schedule, actual monetary loss or automatic reinstatement from the omitted designation. Ragsdale's postdischarge request and December release retain their actual dates. Ordinary employer and employee notice obligations remain distinct, and no paid-leave finding is supplied.

### Zahrey v. Coffey — 2002-03-19

[Record](../records/zahrey-v-coffey_merits_2002-03-19.md)

Justice Scalia dissents, joined by Justice Thomas. They would reverse the reinstatement of the reviewed personal damages claim because the asserted due-process boundary was not sufficiently clear in 1996. They do not decide that deliberate fabrication can never violate the Constitution or that a properly presented Fourth Amendment claim necessarily fails.

Mooney, Pyle and Napue concern deliberate falsehood in the adjudication of guilt. Franks and Malley identify a specific seizure-based protection against material deception. Those authorities forcefully condemn the alleged conduct, but the present theory combines investigative fabrication, pretrial rather than postconviction custody, and the same official's later absolutely protected advocacy. Buckley separated immunity from an actionable constitutional injury and expressly reserved the latter. Albright produced distinct approaches to the constitutional source of a prosecution-related injury, not a settled general due-process rule.

The adverse decision on remand in Buckley, 20 F.3d 789, and Rhodes, 939 F.Supp. 1256, demonstrate substantive uncertainty about a closely related theory. The objection is therefore more than the absence of a factual twin. Anderson requires particularized legal clarity; neither the moral wrongfulness of lying nor a later explanation of why the claim should succeed supplies that clarity at the conduct date. The dissenters would not make every circuit disagreement sufficient for immunity, but find this combination of source, injury and capacity uncertainties material. Later Fletcher and Clanton cannot settle the question retrospectively.

Both Justices accept that title and subsequent advocacy do not transform a conceded investigative act into protected advocacy. They also preserve the distinction between lack of absolute immunity and constitutional or damages liability. Their proposed judgment rests on qualified immunity for this pleaded theory, leaves the ultimate scope of a properly presented constitutional claim open, and supplies neither approval of the alleged misconduct nor an extension of judgment to other defendants or excluded claims.

### Hoffman Plastic Compounds, Inc. v. NLRB — 2002-03-27

[Record](../records/hoffman-plastic-compounds-inc-v-nlrb_merits_2002-03-27.md)

**O'Connor, dissenting, joined by Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas.** The dissent would reverse enforcement of this backpay category because it replaces earnings from an employment opportunity federal law made unavailable and Castro obtained through fraudulent verification. Section 10(c)'s public remedial purpose does not permit the Board to restore that lost-work premise in conflict with the independent federal employment scheme. The payment need not itself constitute hiring, and IRCA need not expressly repeal backpay, for that remedial conflict to matter.

Southern Steamship requires the Board to respect the independent prohibition. Sure-Tan's legal-availability limit remains significant despite its different departure facts; the dissent's sufficient ground is the operative immigration scheme and the established original fraud and lack of authorization, not that precedent's sentence alone. ABF addressed misconduct that did not render the underlying employment federally unavailable and preserves only lawful remedial discretion. McKennon's later-discovered employer ground does not authorize compensation premised on employment forbidden by another enactment. Its actual-would-discharge requirement therefore does not resolve this different statutory bar.

The discovery cutoff and denial of reinstatement prevent future employment, but do not remove the defect in replacement wages for the earlier unauthorized position. The majority's concern that employers may exploit this workforce is substantial. The established labor violation, cease-and-desist and notice obligations, and other lawful remedies nevertheless cannot supply authority for this conflicting wage-replacement category. Nor can a public deterrence objective alone overcome the remedial limit.

The present-tense testimony supports continued-use concerns, but the dissent needs no finding that every interim job required new false documents, that the Board induced fraud, or that a particular post-1990 document violation occurred. It does not apply section 1324c to the original hiring. Its proposed reversal preserves NLRA coverage, the employer's violation, other lawful relief and independent immigration enforcement. It decides neither earned wages nor every remedy involving an unauthorized worker. It would return only for implementation of the backpay bar, not a new mitigation hearing or an additional punitive-remedy holding.

### Barnhart v. Walton — 2002-03-27

[Record](../records/barnhart_v_walton_merits_2002-03-27.md)

**Scalia, concurring in part and in the judgment.** Scalia joins Parts I and III, including the formal delegated-regulation ground, permissible duration and timing constructions, qualifications and reversal. He does not join Part II's additional reliance on sustained administrative interpretation. The formal rule and genuine delegation suffice; duration of an agency practice alone should not confer special controlling weight, and the status of old informal documents cannot be assumed. His position does not demand the Court's independent preferred construction or a new multifactor exception to the governing delegation rules.

**Stone-Zsela, concurring.** The Chief joins the Court's permissible-construction holding and adds that the interpretation also survives independent reading. The lasting impairment must produce the inability that the definition describes; expected duration permits timely decisions, but does not require ignoring actual predecision substantial work. Trial-work protection does not itself create initial entitlement. The agency's longstanding, expert reasons deserve substantial persuasive weight because they fit those enacted distinctions, while its statutory rulemaking assignment and operative regulations separately establish actual delegated authority. This additional ground changes neither the Court's permission into a command nor the stated entitlement qualifications.

### Mickens v. Taylor — 2002-03-27

[Record](../records/mickens_v_taylor_merits_2002-03-27.md)

**Stevens, dissenting.** Stevens would reverse the habeas denial and secure a new trial because the State appointed the murder victim's recent lawyer in a capital case without disclosure or the accused's informed acceptance, despite the judge's knowledge. The right to a meaningful lawyer-client relationship, counsel's disclosure obligation and the court's protective duty operate together. This exceptional State-created arrangement warrants presumed impairment under Cronic; Holloway, Sullivan and Wood supply the conflict safeguards, while Swidler preserves continuing confidentiality without proving any particular confidence. His rule concerns this undisclosed capital victim-counsel appointment, not every former-client relationship or ethical breach. He does not require another actual-conflict hearing before recognizing the constitutional entitlement.

**Souter, dissenting.** Souter would reverse and require a new trial under the court's prospective duty to address a sufficiently identified risk before representation unfolds. When the judge already has adequate notice, Holloway's preventive protection should not depend solely on an objection from a potentially conflicted lawyer. The judge must inquire, secure an intelligent waiver or arrange replacement when warranted. Souter distinguishes that prospective duty from later discovery: he reads Wood's retrospective inquiry as requiring actual conflict and adverse effect, rather than supporting an actual-conflict-only remand here. His discussion of possible adverse effects supplies no decision of the factual issue outside the grant and no direction for another hearing.

**Breyer, dissenting, joined by Ginsburg.** These Justices would reverse and secure a new trial on a narrower structural ground. The State supplied the recent criminal lawyer of the murder victim to defend the accused in a capital case; the immediate incompatibility, the capital proceeding's use of the victim's character and the State's creation of the arrangement produce a breakdown in the trial framework. Cronic and Fulminante support relief without reconstructing individual performance effects at that conjunction of facts. Young v. United States ex rel. Vuitton reinforces the concern for institutional fairness. They expressly leave the general relationship among Holloway, Sullivan and Wood unresolved and do not join Souter's general known-risk rule or an actual-conflict-only remand.

Each dissent seeks the new-trial remedy through ordinary conditional habeas relief, permitting the State to retry with constitutionally adequate representation. None orders immediate unconditional release, acquittal or a new sentence alone. Their constitutional positions do not adjudicate an independently presented collateral defense on predicates absent from the question decided. Counsel maintained the narrower actual-conflict theory; the dissenters give their own legal answers to the granted conflict question and the requested new-trial remedy.

### Steele v. Hamilton County Community Mental Health Board — 2002-03-27

### Adams v. Florida Power Corp. — 2002-04-01

[Record](../records/adams_v_florida_power_corp_dig_2002-04-01.md)

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela dissents. He would decide the certified statutory question, reverse the categorical bar and remand. The district court actually entered and certified that legal ruling, and neither it nor the circuit majority adopted a finding that the impact allegations were insufficient. The unassessed factual merits therefore do not prevent correcting the legal bar. He reads section 623(a)(2)'s effects-oriented classification language together with section 623(f)(1)'s reasonable-factors-other-than-age provision to permit a claim directed to a specifically identified neutral practice that causes an adverse age effect. The employee must identify that practice and establish causation; aggregate workforce disparity alone is insufficient. A proven reasonable nonage basis may justify the differentiation without showing that the practice was the only possible business choice. Hazen Paper reserved impact and does not foreclose that rule. The 1991 Title VII amendments do not justify importing their entire business-necessity scheme into the ADEA. On remand, the lower courts would assess the actual practice, causation, reasonable-factor justification and procedural requirements; recognizing the theory would not itself restore a class or establish liability. This is his dissenting position, not the Court's statutory holding.

### Venetian Casino Resort, L.L.C. v. Local Joint Executive Board of Las Vegas — 2002-04-01

[Record](../records/venetian_casino_resort_v_local_joint_executive_board_merits_2002-04-01.md)

Justice Scalia dissents, joined by Justice Thomas. They would reverse and remand because governmental responsibility for this speech exclusion has not been established. Their framework requires attribution before forum classification: Lebron's concrete structural predicate and the limits emphasized in Brentwood do not allow public benefit, traffic regulation or access covenants alone to substitute for governmental control. The agreement expressly preserves present private rights and requires dedication if the present walkway is later removed, altered or abandoned. In their view, that distinction shows that the State secured passage and safety while withholding a present public speech entitlement. The fact that the walkway replaces a former public route makes the contrary argument substantial, but does not supply control over the disputed exclusion. They do not independently classify the forum after rejecting attribution. Their proposed remand would permit ordinary disposition under that attribution rule, rather than direct arrests, award damages or resolve a taking.

### Ashcroft v. Free Speech Coalition, No. 00-795 — 2002-04-16

[Record](../records/ashcroft_v_free_speech_coalition_merits_2002-04-16.md)

**Thomas, concurring in the judgment.** Thomas agrees with both invalidations but does not join the Court's principal reasoning. Enforcement of actual-child prohibitions is the Government's strongest concern. The present predictive showing does not establish a technological proof obstacle sufficient for this breadth, but practical impossibility of distinguishing fabricated from actual-child images could present a different constitutional question. An appropriate affirmative defense or another narrowly drawn restriction might matter on that demonstrated premise. He neither requires a particular future defense nor approves shifting the actual-child element through a current presumption. His agreement in judgment does not endorse a categorical foreclosure of every enforcement-based restriction affecting protected expression.

**O'Connor, concurring in the judgment in part and dissenting in part; Scalia joins Part II of her writing only.** In Part I she agrees with invalidation of the youthful-adult application: adult performers' protected expression remains exposed because the limited adult defense does not reach ordinary possession. She separately agrees that (D)'s presentation category lacks an adequately justified compelling fit, particularly when describing adult performers as minors removes the defense. In Part II she would preserve (B) for wholly virtual images virtually indistinguishable from actual-child images. The narrow construction excludes cartoons, sculptures and plainly unreal depictions. Congress may respond to supported enforcement predictions before actual proof failure becomes universal; in her view the child-protection interest and limited application satisfy the constitutional inquiry. Broadrick and application-severability principles permit preserving that application without extending the adult defense to possession. Her vagueness conclusion is confined to the realistic-image construction. Scalia joins only this virtual-image sustaining ground, not her adult or presentation invalidations.

**Scalia, dissenting.** Scalia would sustain both definitions under narrowing constructions. The statutory conduct requirement reaches actual or realistically simulated hardcore sexual acts, not merely a film's subject matter, and apparent minority means realistic images virtually indistinguishable from depictions of actual children. He adopts O'Connor's narrow virtual-image enforcement rationale but does not accept her adult-application invalidation. For (D), he reads offense scienter together with the definition to confine liability to knowing pandering by the responsible actor, excluding innocent downstream possession. Ginzburg supports the relevance of deliberate presentation to this confined transaction in his view. The Court regards that reading as alteration of a definition used in connected offenses and finds the remaining protected reach substantial. Scalia does not treat all nonobscene adult expression as unprotected or enlarge §2252A(c)'s defense.

### United States v. Craft, No. 00-1831 — 2002-04-17

[Record](../records/united_states_v_craft_merits_2002-04-17.md)

**Thomas, dissenting, joined by Stevens and Scalia.** Section 6321 reaches property belonging to the taxpayer; it cannot create a distinct individual estate from inseparable marital ownership. Michigan gives the husband neither unilateral withdrawal nor a power to sell, partition or redirect the estate. Drye and National Bank of Commerce involved those actual powers over value, and therefore do not settle this case. Joint enjoyment and a veto on conveyance are meaningful benefits, but remain incidents of the marital entity's ownership rather than separately owned assets. The longstanding collection treatment supports this statutory caution; it does not replace the inquiry. The dissent does not make a creditor-exemption label conclusive or declare all restricted, nontransferable interests unreachable. It would affirm the attachment ruling without deciding a different tenancy, completed severance or separate income right.

**Scalia, dissenting, joined by Thomas.** Entireties ownership also gives institutional security to the nondebtor spouse, including a spouse whose domestic contribution yields no separate income. Removing that protection through a new federal property classification counsels particular statutory caution. This additional ground neither finds that Mrs. Craft held that domestic role nor creates a general marital-assets exemption. Stevens joins the common statutory dissent, but not this additional writing.

### A&M Records, Inc. v. Napster, Inc. — 2002-04-23

[Record](../records/a_and_m_records_v_napster_merits_2002-04-23.md)

No separate opinion is filed.

### Michigan v. Environmental Protection Agency — 2002-04-23

[Record](../records/michigan_v_environmental_protection_agency_merits_2002-04-23.md)

**Scalia, concurring in part and dissenting in part, joined by Thomas.** The enacted criterion concerns amounts of pollution contributing to another State's nonattainment or interfering with maintenance. Cheapness of removal measures expense, not that pollution relationship. Congress's express economic techniques permit ways of meeting an authorized result; they do not authorize EPA to redefine the result through cost. MCI and the limiting-criteria analysis in Iowa Utilities Board require those enacted words to constrain agency choice independently; Chevron supplies no power to replace them. Walton's acceptance of reasonable formal implementation does not help when the disputed choice falls outside the statutory criterion. The two would remand the affected cost determination for agency reconsideration under the pollution standard, without imposing a unique significance number or requiring elimination of every molecule of interstate emissions. They would not preserve the same amount merely by renaming its stage.

They join Part II because Train and the actual alternatives distinguish a lawful statewide result from compelled source selection. That join does not approve the challenged cost-derived amounts. Having resolved the rule on statutory grounds, they do not reach the constitutional sufficiency question. Their existing agreement that agency self-restraint cannot cure missing congressional guidance remains unaffected. The dissent preserves the separate geographic and notice corrections and chooses no judicial replacement budget.

### Tahoe-Sierra Preservation Council, Inc. v. Tahoe Regional Planning Agency, No. 00-1167 — 2002-04-23

[Record](../records/tahoe_sierra_preservation_council_v_tahoe_regional_planning_agency_merits_2002-04-23.md)

**Thomas, dissenting, joined by Scalia.** A complete suspension of economically beneficial use for a substantial defined period can take that present use even though ownership and future use remain afterward. Lucas protects the complete-deprivation category, and First English and Lucas's actual-period remedy distinguish whether a taking occurred from how long compensation runs. Later restoration limits compensation; it does not eliminate the earlier complete loss. The majority's whole-interest position recognizes future value, but in the dissent's view that value does not supply usable property during the defined prohibition.

The two would reverse and remand for a sustained complete-deprivation determination, genuine background property or nuisance limitations and compensation for an actual taking within the challenged thirty-two months. They impose neither a numerical minimum duration nor a rule covering every ordinary permit-processing interval. The case-by-case Nevada Class 1–3 single-family approval possibility, unavailable to stream-environment-zone lands and California parcels, requires attention to the owners actually covered. The State must substantiate an antecedent background rule independently prohibiting the use under ordinary proof requirements; a new legislative harm declaration is insufficient. The abandoned Penn Central theory is not restored, and later plans or judicial restraints cannot be attributed to the two enactments through an unproved six-year causal extension. Their remedy concerns the actual established taking period, not presumed permanent value.

### Universal City Studios, Inc. v. Corley — 2002-04-23
