### Completed chunk 6 participation and procedure

### Compaq Computer Corp. v. Commissioner — 2002-05-28

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participated in the submission and decision.

The Fifth Circuit's judgment stands. Further proceedings implement the restored transaction treatment and any necessary statutory computation within the Commissioner's stipulation. The sham-dependent deficiency and negligence penalty may not be reinstated on the rejected ground, and the contingent expense-deduction alternative requires no adjudication.

### Holmes Group, Inc. v. Vornado Air Circulation Systems, Inc. — 2002-06-03

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The Federal Circuit must transfer the appeal to the Tenth Circuit under §1631, with the original notice date's effect preserved. The appeal remains pending for lawful merits review. Under the June 12, 2000 district order, proceedings on Vornado's counterclaim remain stayed; Holmes's second through seventh claims remain conditionally dismissed except to the extent they support the existing injunctive relief. Affirmance of the first-claim judgment would make the preliminary injunction permanent, discharge the bond, and make those dismissals and dismissal of the counterclaim with prejudice. Reversal would permit either party to seek reinstatement of conditionally dismissed claims by motion showing that reversal. Today's jurisdictional disposition triggers neither branch and does not automatically reopen the ITC proceeding.

### SEC v. Zandford — 2002-06-03

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The case returns for further proceedings on the reviewed §10(b)/Rule 10b-5 claim. The lower court must evaluate the summary-judgment evidence and any necessary-issue preclusion from the wire-fraud judgments, then determine liability and authorized relief under the corrected statutory connection. The statutory ruling itself fixes no disgorgement amount, injunction or civil penalty. The unreviewed §17(a) issue and disclaimed excessive-trading theory receive no new disposition.

### United States v. Mackby — 2002-06-03

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participated at submission and decision.

The Ninth Circuit's remand remains in force. The district court must develop the record as needed and assess whether the combined fixed penalties and treble damages are grossly disproportionate to the gravity of Mackby's established false-claims offense. Each component may be considered in light of the other and its compensatory work. The liability finding is not reopened, and neither a reduction nor a particular constitutional maximum is predetermined.

### Chevron U.S.A. Inc. v. Echazabal — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The case returns to the Ninth Circuit for ordinary appellate consideration of the unresolved medical-evidence issue and any properly preserved remaining question. The motion's assumed disability status, the absence of an accommodation argument against Chevron and Irwin's distinct judgment remain as described above. The Court determines neither Chevron's factual entitlement to the defense nor the ultimate employment or monetary relief.

### Devlin v. Scardelletti — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The Fourth Circuit must consider Devlin's preserved objections to approval under ordinary appellate requirements. The affirmed denial of intervention is not reopened, and the settlement's substantive validity and any pension remedy remain for the proper merits determination.

### Franconia Associates v. United States — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The Federal Circuit judgments return for proceedings on the two reviewed contract components and the Franconia taking component under their distinct rules. Each contract claim requires its own effective election, promised performance and breach chronology, six-year filing interval and remaining merits defenses; the Franconia taking requires its own protected-interest and completed-injury inquiry. The reviewed Grass Valley contract claims belong to Margorie W. Kassner; Eileen R. Kothe; NRCB Limited Partnership, only as to its December 31, 1975 loan; Peters, Williams & Kubota; Pine Needle Apartments Limited Partnership; Richard and Beatrice Schmidtbauer; and The Highlands, a Limited Partnership. ABCD Trust's unresolved loan classification and NRCB's other loans are outside this disposition. Grass Valley Terrace, Jefferson Commons, Ripon Manor, Rural Community Builders and Waterloo Glen were not the identified appellants and petitioners. Post-1979 claims retain their existing lower-court status. The January 2, 2002 Grass Valley taking order remains in its separate procedural posture; this mandate neither reopens nor directly alters it.

### JPMorgan Chase Bank v. Traffic Stream (BVI) Infrastructure Ltd. — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The Second Circuit resumes consideration of the preserved matters left unresolved by its jurisdictional ruling. The BVI corporate relationship requires no further factual remand. Other applicable jurisdictional requirements and the remaining financing issues retain their own operation; neither party consent nor this ruling supplies a new debt judgment.

### McKune v. Lile — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The affirmed injunction protects against the specified consequences imposed solely for refusing unprotected incriminating disclosures. Kansas retains the options of voluntary treatment, revised disclosure requirements, constitutionally adequate protection and independently supported classification. The district court rejected the distinct penile-testing privacy claim, and the appellate treatment followed its Fifth Amendment disposition; this affirmance supplies no new disposition of that separate claim. The criminal judgment and sentence remain unchanged.

### National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Morgan — 2002-06-10

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The lower courts must classify the practices, identify timely acts and determine actual environmental continuity, then address preserved evidentiary and instructional objections and their effect on the vacated judgments. Background proof and independent recovery remain distinct. Ordinary proof of causation, severity or pervasiveness, attribution, loss and any equitable defense remains necessary. The appellate court must determine the proper further proceedings under these rules instead of automatically reinstating the defense verdict or ordering retrial of barred discrete claims.

### United States v. Bollin — 2002-06-10

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participated at submission and decision.

The case returns for removal of the invalid §853(e) restraint and lawful disposition of the release motion. A distinct postconviction restraint or substitute-forfeiture request must establish its actual statutory authority and predicates. The possibility of eventual forfeiture cannot continue the invalid existing order. The broader appellate-counsel question and the unpreserved intermediary issue remain outside this decision.

### United States v. Faasse — 2002-06-10

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participated at submission and decision.

The Sixth Circuit's judgment remains in force. Federal enforcement rests on the actual original-Act elements and leaves creation and modification of the underlying support duty to State law.

### United States v. Farmer — 2002-06-10

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participated at submission and decision.

The district court must conduct a prompt focused adversary hearing. Farmer must establish by a preponderance that the particular funds needed for counsel are untainted and lack probable cause supporting continued forfeiture custody, and that he actually needs them. The government may contest other resources and present probable cause of taint. Success requires access to the legitimate amount needed unless an independently established lawful basis prevents release; failure supplies no release on this counsel claim. The ultimate forfeiture burden remains unchanged.

### Barnes v. Gorman — 2002-06-17

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The case returns for proceedings implementing the punitive exclusion. Compensation and the unreviewed liability determination remain intact; no punitive-culpability or amount-review proceeding is required under these statutory channels. Other independently available relief retains its own authority and conditions.

### Watchtower Bible & Tract Society of New York, Inc. v. Village of Stratton — 2002-06-17

All nine Justices participated at argument and decision.

The lower courts must provide relief against the reviewed advance-identification and permit prerequisite, including dependent carry/display enforcement, for ordinary noncommercial religious and political canvassing and handbill distribution. The narrowed ordinance supplies the operative setting. Section 116.07, owner exclusion, fraud and trespass rules remain enforceable on their own terms. Distinct commercial, fundraising and financing-disclosure applications remain outside this limited adjudication.

## 6. Blockers and Revalidation Needs

No unresolved adjudicative blocker remains in completed chunks 1–7. The remaining 15 scheduled inventory matters retain their calendar positions. The two original-action carryovers and Nebraska decree administration retain their stated stages. Repository-history verification remains with the operator and supplies no judicial condition.

## 7. Source and Research Cutoff

The opening legal baseline remains June 28, 2001. Each completed current-term rule and published position enters only on its Record's effective date; the latest completed event is 2002-06-20. The [chunk 5 public-law reading copy](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK5_PUBLIC_LAW.md) contains mechanically copied bounded Public Projections. Records retain source support, individual cutoffs and preparation limits. The [chunk 6 public-law reading copy](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK6_PUBLIC_LAW.md) copies the new completed Public Projections without adding doctrine. This integration itself admits no external event. Future Run stages select date-eligible authority, excluding uncoordinated same-day decisions, and refresh entering-law slices before modeling.

The [chunk 7 public-law reading copy](../entering-law/OT_2001CHUNK7_PUBLIC_LAW.md) mechanically copies the completed Public Projections. Each retains its own effective date and the common preceding baseline for uncoordinated same-day matters.
